## Grand Prix Washington DC 2013

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6f/90/6f90a8c07666b012419b36b34b11370d9d0824284f22f325309899d039904031.jpg)

No. 22 Owen Turtenwald has been to a dozen Grand Prix Top 8s and left without a trophy every time—until now. Winning in the same city as his previous best GP finish, Turtenwald took down Jared Boettcher in a tense finals matchup that saw the pro pilot UWR [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets) past Sneak and Show and a bevy of highly ranked players in the Top 8.

The former player of the year has been so close so many times that it seemed inevitable he would hold the trophy aloft one day. One day turned out to be this day as Turtenwald used a bevy of counterspells, removal, and a certain [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis) to fight through a field of nearly 1,700.

Turtenwald's big breakthrough wasn't the only story at the largest North American Legacy Grand Prix ever. No. 10 Sam Black continued his incredible run with yet another Top 8, likely predicating a climb up the rankings and into the Player of the Year discussion. No. 20 Craig Wescoe continued to prove himself the master of attacking with White creatures, and Boettcher proved he wasn't "just some random," but a talented player who's now on his way to the Pro Tour.

And D.C. also represents the big, successful Pro-level debut of [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis), which had people asking "what's my name" all weekend long.

But the name everyone will remember from this event is Owen Turtenwald, platinum pro, 22nd ranked player in the world, and now, for the first time, Grand Prix Champion!



## Top 8 Player profiles

 

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7c/bc/7cbc18e48934a091b82b62ebf58b8b8263653c59447e95c79968b8ce2c3acc94.jpg)

 

 

### Drew Tunison

**Age:** 28  
**Hometown:** Brown Mills, NJ  
**Occupation:** Manager  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
 Top 8 StarCityGames Legacy Open

**What deck did you play and why? Would you make any changes to your deck?**  
 Dredge, because.

**Was there anything you saw this weekend that you didn't expect?**  
 No.

**What was the most interesting/craziest/most noteworthy play that you saw or accomplished this weekend?**  
 Opponent resolved [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell), and I put [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) into play.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/37/11/37118a0c2c136d772d11c3e3174c0fc24d6b4286fb85532b59330e39969b1583.jpg)

 

 

### Andre Cuneo

**Age:** 38  
**Hometown:** Philadelphia, PA  
**Occupation:** Computer Programmer  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
 2nd Pro Tour New York City 2000

**What deck did you play and why? Would you make any changes to your deck?**  
 Elves because Reid Duke lent it to me.

**Was there anything you saw this weekend that you didn't expect?**  
 MUD

**What was the most interesting/craziest/most noteworthy play that you saw or accomplished this weekend?**  
 Lost on turn three to an infinite combo with [Staff of Domination](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Staff+of+Domination)

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7f/d9/7fd9363aa1fc4c096a7739ba6e85bd7935afda33047bcf6f2e0c55820518c2a7.jpg)

 

 

### Deshaun Baylock

**Age:** 23  
**Hometown:** Kalamazoo, MI  
**Occupation:** PNC Sales; Team Perfect Storms Member  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
 Top 64 StarCityGames Columbus

**What deck did you play and why? Would you make any changes to your deck?**  
 Esper Stoneblade: [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict) and [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis). [Leyline of Sanctity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Leyline+of+Sanctity): No matter what, you're not beating a good Storm player.

**Was there anything you saw this weekend that you didn't expect?**  
[Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) Tendrils. End of turn, untap beat Double [Meddling Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meddling+Mage), [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique), and double Flusterstorm.

**What was the most interesting/craziest/most noteworthy play that you saw or accomplished this weekend?**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/5b/bb/5bbb1830c1a6aaba041617ec7c54ebca810515d8760b2486e1bbf1778c24c3c9.jpg)

 

 

### Jared Buettcher

**Age:** 21  
**Hometown:** Rensselaer, NY  
**Occupation:** Student, grinder, works at Pastime *Legends*  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
 Top 16 Grand Prix Chicago and Grand Prix Columbus

**What deck did you play and why? Would you make any changes to your deck?**  
 Sneak and Show because [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) is the best mana accelerant still legal.  
 I would have to think; maybe add more [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm)s.

**Was there anything you saw this weekend that you didn't expect?**  
 Not that I played against.I was on the play after my Charbelcher opponent mulliganed to five. I cast a turn one [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) for [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) and my opponent says "Good game." and scoops.

**What was the most interesting/craziest/most noteworthy play that you saw or accomplished this weekend?**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/89/ad/89adcf9bd9498acb4c28bf5e1326c259a8b26a19e326ab95a56a94245d37a800.jpg)

 

 

### Ted McCluskie

**Age:** 24  
**Hometown:** Ottawa, Ontario  
**Occupation:** Student, card store employee  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
 Several PTQ Top 8s; FNM Champion at least twice

**What deck did you play and why? Would you make any changes to your deck?**  
 Shardless BUG.  
 For the most part I'm happy with the cards. [Golgari Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Charm) was bananas.

**Was there anything you saw this weekend that you didn't expect?**  
 No. Everything was pretty stock.

**What was the most interesting/craziest/most noteworthy play that you saw or accomplished this weekend?**  
 In practicing with friends I learned that [Baleful Strix](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baleful+Strix) can kill [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Emrakul%252C%2Bthe%2BAeons%2BTorn), the Aeon's Torn. Cool beans!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/68/25/68256e461450d3d47b3d50b7774753658ef9739d8a256e839f05978f71dd9c30.jpg)

 

 

### Owen Turtenwald

**Age:** 24  
**Hometown:** Milwaukie, WI  
**Occupation:** **Magic**ian  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
 Player of the Year, Pro Tour Top 8, 13 Grand Prix Top 8s

**What deck did you play and why? Would you make any changes to your deck?**  
 I played [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets) because I prepared with it the most and it fits my playstyle. I also think it gives me a fair shot at win any match I play.

**Was there anything you saw this weekend that you didn't expect?**  
 Turn 1 kill with my [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets) deck. Cast [Grafdigger's Cage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grafdigger%27s+Cage) versus Manaless Dredge and he instantly conceded.

**What was the most interesting/craziest/most noteworthy play that you saw or accomplished this weekend?**  
 Beat a resolved [Blood Moon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Moon) when the only card in my deck that could deal damage was [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/20/51/20510b3202a530b09b57fd3096abb8f80190891ded73ab1401a2d00a84147a20.jpg)

## Quarterfinals - Drew Tunison vs. Ted McCluskie

*by Blake Rasmussen*

 

The brackets for the Quarterfinals broke in a strange way for this Top 8, with all of the ranked and practiced pros finding themselves paired against one another in the top side of the bracket while the newcomers and grinders found themselves paired on the lower end.

Two such grinders, New Jersey native Drew Tunison and Canadian Ted McCluskie, faced an interesting match in the quarterfinals. Tunison was playing Dredge, a format boogeyman that many players simply weren't adequately prepared for. McCluskie, meanwhile, was playing Shardless BUG and banking on some of the graveyard hate he was packing—including main deck [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman)—to get him through an otherwise tough match.

### Game 1

[Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) stops Tunison's attempt at a [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting) hoping to turn off his discard outlets immediately...and succeeding. Tunison was forced to pass his second turn while McCluskie suspended an [Ancestral Vision](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancestral+Vision) and started getting his attacks on with [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf).

The move gave Mccluskie plenty of move to maneuver over the next few turns and Tunison struggled to get anything going. It took just a few attacks from a pair of [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) to settle the first game without much of a fuss.

### McCluskie 1 – Tunison 0

![](https://i.ormos.uk/2f/eb/2feb2dd6a46cc1a9a0932bb263e46938c8a3d17e0abc72e80865fbaea786febe.jpg)  
*Ted McCluskie* 

### Game 2

Opening with a bold, blind [Cabal Therapy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Therapy), Tunison nailed a [Nihil Spellbomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nihil+Spellbomb) that McCluskie was clearly banking on resolving. He had a [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) to disrupt any graveyard shenanigans, but it wasn't nearly as powerful as the artifact he watched fall to the graveyard.

That gave Tunison the opportunity to resolve a pair of [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting)s without much fear, milling 11 cards but failing to hit any [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba)s that would have let him do anything terribly broken.

All McCluskie could do was take away an [Ichorid](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ichorid) from Tunison's graveyard and pass the turn.

Once again Tunison missed on [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba)s during the dredge, but cast one the hard way regardless. That, coupled with [Cabal Therapy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Therapy) and [Dread Return](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread+Return), let Tunison plop a very large, slightly disheartening [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) into play for the Dredge mage.

A few draws, a few more dredges and a [Flame-Kin Zealot](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flame-Kin+Zealot) later and Tunison had evened up the match at a game apiece.

### McCluskie 1 – Tunison 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/af/a1/afa1e5fcc54eea8fe18dd8363c2f25e139312369d5e90c3174de61d3701782bb.jpg)  
*Drew Tunison* 

### Game 3

A pair of [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland)s kept Tunison from accomplishing much in his opening turns as a relatively weak hand from McCluskie left him relying almost solely on a single [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) to stem the tide. But he somewhat oddly declined to use the [Counterspell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterspell) when Tunison offered up a [Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond).

"That was unexpected," the Dredge player said as he used the artifact to help power up a [Cephalid Coliseum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cephalid+Coliseum) sitting in play. Suddenly he was off and running.

![Ichorid](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Ichorid)

Unexpected, and possibly deadly, as [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba) leapt into play and immediately back into the graveyard for a [Cabal Therapy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Therapy) and two Zombie tokens (courtesy of [Bridge from Below](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bridge+from+Below)). McCluskie slumped in his chair a bit as the best he could muster was an [Abrupt Decay](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abrupt+Decay) on a Zombie token.

A few more dredges, a few more Zombies and [Ichorid](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ichorid)s, and Tunison claimed the Quarterfinals.

"Should have Forced the [Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond)," McCluskie said, shaking his head.

"I thought you were going to, but if you did, then I probably got to resolve [Breakthrough](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Breakthrough)."

However, McCluskie figured that [Breakthrough](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Breakthrough) would be far more deadly and wanted to conserve his Force for that should McCluskie have it (he did). McCluskie had to make a choice where neither option was likely to lead to a good situation, and got stuck holding the bag when the Dredge carousel finally ended with a plethora of Zombies.

### Tunison 2 – McCluskie 1

## Quarterfinals - Jared Boettcher vs. Deshaun Baylock

*by Blake Rasmussen*

 

Unfortunately, a deck registration error was discovered in Deshaun Baylock's list before the Top 8, meaning he was starting the match a game down before even shuffling. Given Jared Boettcher's potentially explosive Sneak and Show deck, that didn't bode well for the Michigan native.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/44/d5/44d5ca70f828d9c69baaf02c8cc352aa7c95430707b97b5aecd548a4f11584bd.jpg)  
*Deshaun Baylock* 

The silver lining was that Baylock had already locked up his first Pro Tour invite by way of his Top 8. He had recently come close—several PTQ Top 8 appearances—but fallen just short. Now he was guaranteed a trip to the big show regardless of his game loss, or his result from this point forward.

Baylock is relatively green on the GP scene, but Boettcher has had some success before, finishing in the Top 16 of two Modern GPs, Grand Prix Chicago and Grand Prix Columbus. This is his first Top 8 at a Grand Prix and his first Pro Tour invite as well.

### Game 2, sort of

After the sting of a game loss at the worst possible time, Baylock was under the gun immediately as a mulligan set him back.

"This is nothing new, this is what I do for a living," Baylock said.

"Grind?"

"Game losses."

But Baylock started strong, despite his reluctance with his hand and initial game loss, opening on [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) into a sword of Fire and Ice.

However, it didn't matter too much as Boettcher checked Baylock's hand with [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe), made sure the way was clear, and [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell)ed an [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn) into play on turn two off [Ancient Tomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Tomb). When Baylock had no way to interact with the Eldrazi Legend and no way to keep his board intact through an attack, Baylock made a quick exit from the Top 8, but not before picking up his first Pro Tour invite.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/84/16/8416326dc346d983979f4536935c3eaecab9ffb59b3dc472725d5254679811cc.jpg)  
*Jared Boettcher* 

Boettcher liked his matchup this round, even if he did get a little help from outside forces. He said Esper Stoneblade was a strong pre-board match and a close post-board match. He was, however, worried about both Dredge and Death and Taxes. Fortunately for him, Death and Taxes was in the other bracket.

### Boettcher 2 – Baylock 0

## Quarterfinals - (22) Owen Turtenwald vs. Andrew Cuneo

*by Adam Styborski*

 

For the premier level success Owen Turtenwald, ranked 22nd in the world, has seen there's one win he hasn't been able to earn: Grand Prix champion. The former Player of the Year was playing Blue-White-Red [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets), something he said he felt confident and comfortable with.

Andrew Cuneo is on the same resurgent path that many "old school" players have taken in recent years. Jon Finkel and Brian Kibler still play the game at the highest levels, and newly minted Pro Tour Hall of Famer William "Huey" Jensen picked up a fresh Grand Prix win this year in Oakland. For Cuneo, taking the deck of No. 3-ranked Reid Duke - Elves - has paid off with a chance to claim modern **Magic** glory.

Cuneo opened into a handful of removal from Turtenwald: [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) and two copies of [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) sent [Fyndhorn Elves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fyndhorn+Elves), [Dryad Arbor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dryad+Arbor) and [Quirion Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Quirion+Ranger) packing. With [Elvish Visionary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Elvish+Visionary) in play Cuneo took a long time to think after Turtenwald played [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic).

[Nettle Sentinel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nettle+Sentinel), [Glimpse of Nature](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glimpse+of+Nature), [Heritage Druid](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Heritage+Druid) and [Wirewood Symbiote](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wirewood+Symbiote) all followed. As he drew then another Symbiote, [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman), and [Natural Order](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Natural+Order) for [Craterhoof Behemoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Craterhoof+Behemoth). Turtenwald lost his [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) and fell to one life in blocking. A final draw step didn't provide what he needed to survive.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/06/f7/06f738faa82dd5098971accf7e3d94831644ad3e913b6b12fa9c67b66095f4ed.jpg)  
*Cuneo was hoofing it to finish the match quickly. It wouldn't go quite to plan.* 

Turtenwald led off the second game with [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer), a utility creature tha could threaten to eat nearly every creature in Cuneo's deck. Turtenwald did just that to Cuneo's [Fyndhorn Elves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fyndhorn+Elves) after using [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) on his second turn. Cuneo had plenty more, adding both [Wirewood Symbiote](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wirewood+Symbiote) and [Quirion Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Quirion+Ranger) on his second turn - though Turtenwald had [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) and a fetch land to keep the battlefield clear.

After Turtenwald played [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) for [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte), the game began to slow. [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) and [Quirion Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Quirion+Ranger) allowed Cuneo to use multiple [Gaea's Cradle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaea%27s+Cradle)s to power out a [Natural Order](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Natural+Order), but Turtenwald had [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) waiting. With two [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland)s Turtenwald stripped away Cuneo's mana, and an uncontested [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte) sealed away any other Elf creatures Cuneo might try.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/95/c2/95c2040b960961c84008d10105186534d8a8e6494503b35ae15219ff757126fb.jpg)  
*Turtenwald always had what he needed in the second and third games.* 

"I don't know why you played the Force that game but not the first," Cuneo said.

With a shrug and a grin, Turtenwald offered a simple response: "I forgot."

Cuneo's opening of Forest, [Fyndhorn Elves](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fyndhorn+Elves) was familiar, and so was Turtenwald's [Volcanic Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Island) into [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt). [Quirion Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Quirion+Ranger) and [Gaea's Cradle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaea%27s+Cradle) followed, but a lonely [Grafdigger's Cage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grafdigger%27s+Cage) was all Turtenwald mustered.

[Glimpse the Unthinkable](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glimpse+the+Unthinkable) was Cuneo's first play in the third turn, but when Cuneo played [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) Turtenwald used his second [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) on [Quirion Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Quirion+Ranger). Cuneo had another Ranger to replace it, but it also stopped Cuneo from following through on further action.

Turtenwald's [Meddling Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meddling+Mage) locking out [Glimpse of Nature](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glimpse+of+Nature) cut off another angle, and [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) for [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte) seemed to make things impossible for Cuneo. But with multiple creatures in play and an extra [Gaea's Cradle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaea%27s+Cradle) in hand, Cuneo attempted a hard-cast [Craterhoof Behemoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Craterhoof+Behemoth).

[Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) was there again for Turtenwald. Stuck with an uncastable [Progenitus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Progenitus) and [Glimpse of Nature](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glimpse+of+Nature) in hand, Cuneo was forced to block away his [Quirion Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Quirion+Ranger). With counters on his [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte), Turtenwald equipped [Meddling Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meddling+Mage) and methodically marched across everything else Cuneo played. Owen Turtenwald advanced to his semifinal match against Sam Black.

William Jensen, who was hung over my shoulder the entire match, started discussing the result with Cuneo and Turtenwald.

"He beat me game 2 with [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) because my draws were bad," Cuneo explained to Jensen. "I'm not sure if I should have mulliganed Game 2 \[instead of keeping\]. I had [Gaea's Cradle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaea%27s+Cradle), [Gaea's Cradle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaea%27s+Cradle) and guys."

"My hand game three was great, "Turtenwald offered. "I had [Grafdigger's Cage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grafdigger%27s+Cage), [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), Meddling, [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm), a fetch, and Tunda. It was great."

The right tools always make the difference against combo decks.

## Quarterfinals - (20) Craig Wescoe vs. (10) Sam Black

*by Event Coverage Staff*

 

![](https://magic.wizards.com/sites/all/themes/wiz_mtg/img/interface/video-place-holder.png)[.](http://www.youtube.com/embed/Za_8ZkadHCw)

 

## Semifinals - Jared Boettcher vs. Drew Tunison

*by Blake Rasmussen*

 

Before this round, Jared Boettcher worried about playing against exactly two decks: Death and Taxes and Dredge. Death and Taxes was stuck on the other side of the bracket and wouldn't matter until a potential finals match.

So I'll give you one guess what Boettcher's semifinals opponent Drew Tunison was playing.

Tunison had dredged his way through a tournament that, apparently, had skimped on the hate enough to let the graveyard-based deck make it through to the Top 8. It was the kind of deck that was easily hated out—but only if players tried hard enough.

Boettcher, however, was playing a pretty dangerous deck himself. He won his Quarterfinals match with a turn two [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn), a start even Dredge might not be able to keep up with. However, hilariously enough, Tunison had [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand)s in his deck that could make [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) potentially awkward for the Red Blue player.

All told, there was a lot of play and a number of ways this match could go. Only one way to find out which way it would be...

### Game 1

Tunison was quick to throw back his first few hands, but, with Dredge, sometimes it didn't matter much.

"I've mulled to two and won this weekend," Tunison said.

"What turn did you win? Tell me it was like turn one. Even lie to me," Boettcher said.

Tunison thought a bit, looked at another hand he mulliganed and said "I think it was turn two."

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f0/23/f0239fb36007e4a6e4b91741a4f4cf21aed2c3ad4f31785635bc7b1bf2c7d038.jpg)  
*Jared Boettcher* 

He would need a little bit of that magic again as Tunison went all the way down to two cards again. [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe) revealed that those two cards were [Cephalid Coliseum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cephalid+Coliseum) and [Dread Return](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread+Return)—no one's definition of winning any time soon.

And when Boettcher used [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) to put a second-turn [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) into play—facing down a big, bad [Golgari Thug](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Thug)—we were quickly on to the second game.

### Boettcher 1 – Tunison 0

### Game 2

"I will keep," Tunison proclaimed happily.

Boettcher, aware that wasn't good news for him, mouthed something I can't print. Suffice to say, he was less than thrilled.

![Cephalid Coliseum](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Cephalid+Coliseum)

And the reason why was apparent right away, as Tunison gave him a difficult decision, playing [Cephalid Coliseum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cephalid+Coliseum) and [Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond).

"Oh, one of these conundrums? I heard what happened last time," Boettcher said, opting to use [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) on the powerful artifact.

That successfully set Tunison back a turn, forcing him to instead rely on [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting) and [Breakthrough](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Breakthrough) to get going. [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting) resolved. [Breakthrough](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Breakthrough) did not.

As if on cue, Boettcher drew [Lotus Petal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Petal), enabling him to play [Sneak Attack](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sneak+Attack). After [Forcing](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) twice in the early turns, that left him with exactly one card—fortunately for Boettcher, that card was [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand). He was all set to go off with [Sneak Attack](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sneak+Attack) the following turn.

Unfortunately for Boettcher, Tunison had a pair of [Cabal Therapies](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Therapy+) to remove [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) as a threat and give his deck the breathing room it needed to gear up.

More Dredging, more Zombie tokens and a [Cephalid Coliseum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cephalid+Coliseum) activation later and Tunison had his own [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) in play and nearly his entire library in the graveyard. Given that that's the point of the deck, Tunison was able to find a way to attack for the win with [Flame-Kin Zealot](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flame-Kin+Zealot).

### Tunison 1 – Boettcher 1

### Game 3

"I like being the rando," Tunison said, referring to being the two unranked players left in this tournament.

"I like being the rando, too," Boettcher said. "I just hope I'm the rando holding the trophy at the end of the day."

Tunison and Boettcher may not have the pedigree that No. 10 Sam Black and No. 22 Owen Turtenwald have on the other side of the bracket, but one of them would definitely be playing for that trophy at the conclusion of this match. And if they take the trophy, they'll certainly be less random than they see themselves now.

Boettcher staked an early lead on achieving that, resolving a [Grafdigger's Cage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grafdigger%27s+Cage) that would force Tunison to find an answer before he could do anything too broken.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/4c/c3/4cc373de5bf46fa7ea5644c021e97dd309ace944f93f344826f9feb6819635dd.jpg)  
*Drew Tunison* 

Instead, Boettcher attempted to do something broken of his own, resolving [Sneak Attack](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sneak+Attack) on his third turn and placing [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) in play on his fourth, immediately drawing seven cards. His first seven missed on an [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Emrakul%252C%2Bthe%2BAeons%2BTorn), the Aeon's Torn and he declined to go fishing with seven more. With a hand full of [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), [Misdirection](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misdirection), [Divert](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Divert) and [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm), he had pretty much everything anyway. There was little Tunison could do.

The second [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) the next turn found [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn) and sent Boettcher onto the finals with Sneak and Show where he'll represent the "randos" against the "pros."

### Boettcher 2 – Tunison 1

## Semifinals - (10) Sam Black vs. (22) Owen Turtenwald

*by Event Coverage Staff*

 

![](https://magic.wizards.com/sites/all/themes/wiz_mtg/img/interface/video-place-holder.png)[.](http://www.youtube.com/embed/wEzaxOSs4bU)

 

## Finals - Jared Boettcher vs. (22) Owen Turtenwald

*by Adam Styborski*

 

Three years.

What's the blink of an eye in life itself is an eternity for **Magic**. It was three years ago in 2010 that Owen Turtenwald lost in the finals of Grand Prix Washington D.C.to Brad Nelson. Over those three years, Turtenwald won a Player of the Year title, earned two Pro Tour Top 8s and several more Grand Prix Top 8s, and is now ranked 22nd in the world but had yet to lift the victory trophy at a Grand Prix.

It may not be the flashiest feather to add to Turtenwald's very-well-feathered cap, but it's one he's desperately wanted. "How long until we can start?" he asked as he waited for the video coverage to kick in. He was eager to make his move.

Jared Boettcher was in his first Grand Prix Top 8, and was close to closing out the dream of everyone who entered the event: ultimate victory. Having secured his invitiation to Pro Tour *Born of the Gods* by making the Top 8 of the largest North American Legacy Grand Prix. Earning quick victories in his quarterfinal and semifinal matches reiterated the potency of Sneak and Show: It was the boogeyman that everyone knew about coming into the weekend.

Boettcher, seeded higher, had the luxury to lead off with a [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe) and peek at what Turtenwald was holding: [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) and [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) were the highlights Boettcher needed to know. A turn later a second Gitaxian probe added [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) to Boettcher's potential delays. Turtenwald's early [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver+of+Secrets) transformed, but before going to combat Boettcher tried for [Intuition](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intuition). [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) and [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) from Turtenwald put a stop to that. Turtenwald used his [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) on Boettcher's [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder), and had drawn a second copy of the counterspell to go alongside an additional [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will).

An uncontested [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver+of+Secrets) eats life at an alarming rate. Normally a fast game of Legacy means the combo deck found success. Here, Boettcher's life ran out long before Turtenwald's countermagic.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/43/30/4330499edd1b5d0a4e72eaa4632151078f304aa5b6906b599717ba1af93711db.jpg)  
*Jarad Boettcher was stymied by Own Turtenwald's [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) at every turn.* 

"You had a really good hand," Boettcher admitted as they moved on.

![Batterskull](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Batterskull)

The second game started slower, each player playing and passing as they accumulated land. Turtenwald broke the silence with [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic), pulling out [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull).

"You have six cards?" Boettcher asked when Turtenwald ended his turn.

"Seven with [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull)," Turtenwald corrected.

"Ah. You missed a land drop." Boettcher was counting.

Boettcher opened all his fetchlands for a variety of basics and a [Volcanic Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Island). [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe) revealed double [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce), double [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), [Pyroblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroblast), [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull), and [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine). Boettcher didn't waste time before going for [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell).

"Pryoblast." was all Turtenwald needed, and Boettcher ended his turn.

On his next, Boettcher attempted the same with a second [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell). Turtenwald cast [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) with an exiled a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce), and again Boettcher just ended his turn. Slipping, then untapping and equipping [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) led to a discarded [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) from Boettcher's hand, and left Turtenwald with all his mana ready to meet yet another [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) from Boettcher.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3e/ab/3eabc15f61e65fbfbc027e3a1ccc19d98db4e9f371a6de5910713a1d87f20926.jpg)  
*Turtenwald has his eye on the prize the entire match.* 

Turtenwald's second [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), again exiling Spell Peirce, cut Boettcher's move short. [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull) came down soon after, and Boettcher discarded [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn) to Turtenwald's next attack, which had the added benefit of circulating the [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell)s back for Boettcher to draw again.

When Boettcher tried something different, [Sneak Attack](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sneak+Attack), Owen tapped his five lands and hard cast the last card in his hand: A third copy of [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) he could play from the land he drew the previous turn. Boettcher paused for a moment before revealing [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn) as he extended his hand.

"Insane hands," Boettcher said as he shook his head, grinning.

"Yeah," agreed Turtenwald. It was an uncharacteristically quiet response from the newest Grand Prix champion.

The feather Turtenwald wanted was finally fitted in his cap.

## Top 5 Cards

*by Blake Rasmussen*

 

 

![Golgari Charm](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Golgari+Charm)

 

### 5\. [Golgari Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Charm)

When the various *Return to Ravnica* Charms were first released, there was fairly widespread consensus that [Golgari Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Charm) was among the worst of the bunch. Boros, Azorius, Selesnya and even [Rakdos Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Charm) all were hyped for some reason or another.

But when you move into Legacy, [Golgari Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Charm) reigns supreme. Not only does it have some anti-enchantment utility, it's one of the best answers to [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis) in the format. It also happens to be strong against Elves, Goblins and other random one-toughness creatures. The card was instrumental in letting Ted McCluskie fight through a True-Name-heavy field with BUG, and no Black-Green Legacy player should leave home without it.

 

![Green Sun's Zenith](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Green+Sun%27s+Zenith)

 

### 4\. [Green Sun's Zenith](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Green+Sun%27s+Zenith)

Banned in Modern, certainly no one doubts the power of [Green Sun's Zenith](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Green+Sun%27s+Zenith). But the versatility is really what makes it so special. Able to serve as a mana accelerator alongside [Dryad Arbor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dryad+Arbor), a win condition alongside [Craterhoof Behemoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Craterhoof+Behemoth), and a utility tool with any number of creatures, [Green Sun's Zenith](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Green+Sun%27s+Zenith) was instrumental in both (10) Sam Black's Bant deck and Andrew Cuneo's Elf list in the Top 8. Cuneo's deck was made all the more consistent with the sorcery while Black was easily able to search out [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), [Scavenging Ooze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scavenging+Ooze), [Gaddock Teeg](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaddock+Teeg) and more to keep opponents off balance.

 

![Griselbrand](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Griselbrand)

 

### 3\. [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand)

[Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) was once hyped as a format-ending monster that would demand immediate banning once its true power was known. While that never came to be, he has proven to be the defacto-monster, alongside [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Emrakul%252C%2Bthe%2BAeons%2BTorn), the Aeon's Torn, to [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate), Sneak, Show or otherwise cheat into play. Often leading to a game-ending flurry of cards, [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) enables all kinds of strategies. Drew Tunison's Dredge deck, for instance, won a number of games by casting [Dread](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread) return on the Legendary Demon and immediately dredging nearly his entire deck. Other brave players paired the 7/7 with [Children of Korlis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Children+of+Korlis) for a super combo of sorts.

 

![Stoneforge Mystic](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Stoneforge+Mystic)

 

### 2\. [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic)

It's interesting to think what Legacy would look like without [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic). Without it, Death and Taxes may lack the power to go with its taxing ability. Without it, Esper Stoneblade probably wouldn't exist. Without it, the Bant deck No. 10 Sam Black used to continue his incredible run might not exist. Without it, (22) Owen Turtenwald might not have won his first Grand Prix.

Time and again [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) enables so very much for just two mana, including uncounterable [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull)s and ways to win Jitte wars. It's so dangerous Sam Black was willing to set himself back with [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) on the draw just to keep Turtenwald from resolving a copy—twice—in the semifinals. And it was [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) holding [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) as Turtenwald attacked for his first Grand Prix win ever.

[Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) might just be the best card in Legacy. But it's not quite the card of the weekend.

 

![True-Name Nemesis](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=True-Name+Nemesis)

 

### 1\. [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis)

No card was more hyped coming into the weekend and no card did more to justify that hype than [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis). "Baby [Progenitus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Progenitus)" is cheap, difficult to kill, and carries equipment exceedingly well. The first Blue creature to challenge [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) for "best combat-oriented creature" since ever, *Nemesis* proved itself to be a mirror- and pseudo-mirror breaker every step of the way. When Sam Black and Turtenwald faced off in the semifinals, it was [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503154158/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis) that proved to be the difference maker, as Turtenwald resolved his first...and second.

And if you don't believe me, Turtenwald called the 3/1 Merfolk "the real deal." So get used to fearing your True-Name, because it's now one of the most important cards in all of Legacy.
